# nonic-torsion

Code and logs accompanying the paper *Torsion of elliptic curves over nonic fields*, which proves

  Φ(9) = Φ^∞(9) ∪ {Z/29, Z/31, Z/32, Z/33, Z/34, Z/39, Z/40, Z/42, Z/2 × Z/26},

so that exactly 51 groups occur as the torsion subgroup of an elliptic curve over a number field
of degree 9 (the 42 groups of Φ^∞(9), determined by Najman and Varivoda, and 9 further groups),
and that a prime p is the order of a point on an elliptic curve over a number field of degree 9 if
and only if p ≤ 31.

The table in [Which script proves what](#which-script-proves-what) is the authoritative map from
each statement of the paper that rests on a computation to the script that verifies it and the log
that script produced. In the digital version of the paper, the words reporting a computation are
links to the script that carries it out. Most logs can be regenerated with
[`verify_all.sh`](#reproducing-the-computations). Some cannot be regenerated by a single command,
because the run was interrupted, cut by a time limit, continued by hand, or its script was edited
after it ran; they are marked "archived" in `jobs.txt`, and the section
[Archive-only logs](#archive-only-logs) explains each of them. Where such a log carried part of a
proof, the computation was repeated for this repository as one fail-closed batch run, and the new
log is the one the table cites; see [Reruns made for this repository](#reruns-made-for-this-repository).
All other logs are those of the original runs, byte for byte; see
[Provenance of the committed logs](#provenance-of-the-committed-logs).

This repository is organized like those of the quintic, sextic, septic and octic papers
([quintic-torsion](https://github.com/F-Najman/quintic-torsion),
[sextic_torsion](https://github.com/F-Najman/sextic_torsion),
[septic_torsion](https://github.com/F-Najman/septic_torsion),
[octic_torsion](https://github.com/F-Najman/octic_torsion)): one folder per proposition of the paper.

## Layout

Each folder treats one proposition of the proof and is named after the groups it excludes; its
scripts are at the top, its logs in `logs/` and the data its scripts read or export in `data/`
(with sub-folders for the parts of a computation that were kept together, and `degree8/` for the
files of the octic computations that the proof reuses).

|---|---|---|

`original_names.tsv` gives, for every file, its path in the original computational record (relative
to its folder `work/degree9`); the files written by the reruns made for this repository are marked
there as such.

## Getting the code

The Magma code depends on [mdmagma](https://github.com/koffie/mdmagma), which supplies the
moduli-theoretic routines (`MDX1`, `MDX11`, `HeckeOperator`, `DiamondOperator`, `ModuliPoint`,
`CuspOrbitsQ`) and, through its own `Magma` submodule, Sutherland's optimised models of
`X_1(N)` and `X_1(M,N)`. The point counts use Sutherland's
[GL2 package](https://github.com/AndrewVSutherland/Magma). Both are pinned as submodules, at the
same commits as in sextic_torsion, septic_torsion and octic_torsion.

```bash
git clone https://github.com/F-Najman/nonic-torsion
cd nonic-torsion
git submodule update --init                       # mdmagma and gl2
git -C mdmagma submodule update --init Magma      # Sutherland's models, used by mdmagma
git -C mdmagma apply ../mdmagma-magma2.30.patch   # needed with Magma V2.30, see below
```

Do not use `git clone --recurse-submodules`: mdmagma has a further submodule
`tests/magma-unittest` registered under an SSH URL, so a full recursive clone can fail without a
GitHub SSH key. It is not used here.

The pinned commits are mdmagma `d1c019c6` (with its `Magma` submodule at `6a5a68a8`) and the GL2
package at `e70690a`. The latter two are different checkouts of the same repository on purpose:
mdmagma pins a commit that predates `gl2points.m`, so the point-count scripts attach the separate
copy in `gl2/`, and every other script attaches mdmagma's. The original record kept its own copies
of both packages (one in the main software folder and several more inside the folders of single
computations); apart from Magma's signature caches (and a checkout of the unused
`tests/magma-unittest`), every one of these copies agrees file by file with these commits plus the
patch below.

**The mdmagma patch.** Magma V2.30 rejects two statements of the pinned mdmagma: an
`end intrinsic` without its semicolon in `v2/curves/divisors.m` and in
`v2/modular_curves/base_curve.m`. All computations here were run with a copy of mdmagma in which
these two semicolons had been added, and nothing else changed. `mdmagma-magma2.30.patch` makes
exactly this change; apply it with the command above. `verify_all.sh` checks whether it is
applied and applies it if not.

## Running the computations

All scripts are run from the repository root; the `Attach`, `AttachSpec`, `load` and `Read`
paths and the paths of the logs and data files are resolved from there. Everything was run with
Magma V2.30-1. The Python scripts need Python 3 with SymPy; the scripts that import `sage.all`
(the replays named `..._sage.py` and several others) must be run with the Python of a SageMath
installation (we used SageMath's `python3`, which is also the `python3` of the commands in
`jobs.txt`); a few scripts use the M4RI library through Sage. The shell driver requires Bash 4.4 or
later and GNU command-line tools. Magma commands use `-n` to suppress personal startup files.

Several drivers take parameters, passed as `magma -n -b Name:=value script.m`, for example:

* `common/hecke_sieve_quotient_D9.m` — the Hecke sieve on `X_1(N)` in degree `D` with the
  push-forward to the quotients `X_1(M)`, `M` in `BaseNs`, as a prefilter; parameters `N`, `BaseNs`,
  `p`, `Qs`, `D`, `M`, `MemGB`, `Shard`, `NShard`, `FromDegree`, `MaxPart`. A run with `FromDegree`
  or `MaxPart` treats only the partition types of degree at least `FromDegree` and largest part at
  most `MaxPart`, and ends in `SIEVE_RESUME_DONE` instead of `SIEVE_DONE`.
* the drivers of single cases, see `jobs.txt`.

Everything else runs without parameters. The commands that produced the committed logs are in
`jobs.txt`. For example:

```bash
magma -n -b N:=38 BaseNs:=19 p:=3 Qs:=5,7,11,13,17,19 D:=9 M:=1 MemGB:=12 common/hecke_sieve_quotient_D9.m
python3 common/candidates.py
python3 common/structural_completeness_complete_20261027.py
```

Magma may buffer its output, so a terminated run can leave an empty or incomplete log, and some
Magma errors return exit status 0. Check both the exit status and the closing marker: every
script prints a marker when it finishes, and the third column of `jobs.txt` gives it for each
log. Many scripts end without `quit;`; run them with standard input at end of file
(`< /dev/null`), as `verify_all.sh` does, so that Magma exits when the script ends. Scripts that
write their log themselves (`SetLogFile`, or a file opened by the script) are run with their
standard output discarded (`> /dev/null`).

## Reproducing the computations

`jobs.txt` lists each log file, the command that produces it, and its closing marker. An optional
fourth column gives an expected result as an extended regular expression.

```bash
./verify_all.sh                 # every job, Magma jobs one at a time
./verify_all.sh -j 4            # Magma jobs four at a time
./verify_all.sh C45/            # only the jobs whose log path contains C45/
./verify_all.sh -j 4 C2x        # both
python3 -m unittest discover -s tests   # the driver's tests and checks of the committed files
git diff                        # what the rerun changed
```

The tests check the driver and the committed files: the closing markers and expected results of
the committed logs, the files named in the commands and loaded by the scripts, and that every log
and data file kept byte for byte still has the SHA-256 recorded in the original proof ledger
(`common/data/evidence_sha256.tsv`). Run them on the committed tree, before rerunning jobs.

The driver overwrites the selected logs in place; `git diff` compares the new output with the
committed run. It runs the selected Magma jobs first, then the other jobs one at a time and in
the order of `jobs.txt`, because the Python jobs read the logs and data files written by the
Magma jobs. When Magma jobs are selected, the driver first applies the mdmagma patch if
necessary and then attaches every package once, in separate processes, so that parallel jobs do
not compile the same signature files at the same time.

Some scripts write further logs besides their standard output (a driver that runs several Magma
stages, a script with its own log file, the Riemann–Roch identity logs written by the class-group
routines). Such a log is listed in `jobs.txt` with the command `written by the job of LOG`; the
driver checks it after the job of `LOG` has run, and selecting it selects that job.

A job passes only if its command exits 0, its closing line is the expected marker (optionally
followed by a space and details), and any result expression matches. The closing line is the
last non-blank line of the log, ignoring carriage returns, a trailing `Script done on ...` line
written by `script(1)`, and trailing lines that consist of a CPU time only. The markers omit the
CPU times at the end of the closing lines. As Magma reports most errors on standard output,
carries on and exits with status 0, a job also fails if its log reports a Magma error.

`jobs.txt` has 805 lines: 495 Magma jobs, 215 Python jobs, 40 logs written by
another job, and 55 archive-only logs. Rerunning all jobs costs about 31 hours of
CPU time, most of it on the longest computations listed under [Hardware](#hardware).

The jobs that export data rewrite the corresponding files in `data/`; a correct rerun rewrites
them with the same content, with the exceptions below. Some computations choose representatives in
an order that Magma does not fix (a representative of a diamond orbit, a curve in an isomorphism
class, the order of places, random points or test curves, the basis of a class group). A rerun can
therefore print different representatives and write the same records in a different order or
with other random choices. Where later jobs refer to the committed order of such records, rerun an
export only together with the jobs that read it, or restore the committed file with `git checkout`
before rerunning those jobs. This concerns in particular the census records written by
`common/odd_census.m` (`C57`, `C65`, `C85_C91_C93`), the source-unit, Hecke-jet and tensor data of
`C57/source85`, `C65` (`CM6`, `allbad`, `composite65`), `C85_C91_C93` (`C85`, `C91`,
`N93_source_units`) and `C33/F5_rr`, the canonical data of `P73/quotient19` and `P79_P89`, the
direct enumerations and filter exports of `C2x40` and `C2x42` (and the retests of `C2x42`), and the
class-test records of `C48` and the point keys of the `p53` controls of `P43_P53_P61`: in our reproduction pass, 24 checks
that read such exports failed after the exports had been regenerated earlier in the same pass, and
all of them passed when they were rerun on the committed data. Jobs that share an exported file must
not run at the same time; with `-j` greater than 1, run the jobs of one folder at a time if in
doubt. The Riemann–Roch identity logs (`*_RR_identity.log`, `RR.log`, `identities.log`) record
random trials and change from run to run.

What a successful reproduction should change, and nothing else:

* timing figures, including the `time=...` and `cpu=...` fields, the `Time:` and `Total time` lines
  and the bare CPU times;
* Magma's banner and the paths in its `Loading "..."` lines, which name the original working
  directories, and the input paths and SHA-256 hashes that some replays print;
* the choice of representatives, random points and test curves, and the order of records,
  described above;
* whitespace, and whatever a different version of Magma prints differently, such as the printed
  form of a model or an ideal, the order of the elements in a printed set, or the verbose trace of
  a class-group computation.

Everything else is a genuine discrepancy: the place counts, the Hecke primes and cusp conditions,
every `RESULT`, `SURVIVOR` and `SUMMARY` line, the group invariants and class-membership results,
the dimensions and ranks of the Riemann–Roch and formal-immersion tests, and the closing markers.

### Archive-only logs

These logs are kept because they are part of the original record of the proof, but no single
command regenerates them. Each is listed in `jobs.txt` with the command `archived: ...`, and is
not rerun by `verify_all.sh`.

* The original run of `P43_P53_P61/p53_good9_native_probe.m` (the three nonprincipality tests on `X_1(53)_F2`, genus 92, for the three diamond orbits of the 78 noncuspidal places of degree 9). It was started as an exploratory probe under `timeout 1800`; as the limit approached, a guard script stopped the `timeout` process (SIGSTOP), waited for the Magma worker, which was never signalled, to finish, read its exit status (0) and resumed the monitor. The log is complete (closing marker, no error line, all three tests `principal=false`), but its run was not an ordinary batch run. The computation was repeated for this repository with the same script as one fail-closed batch run, `P43_P53_P61/logs/p53_good9_native_probe_rerun.log` (exit status 0, 864 CPU seconds, the same three results), which is the log cited for Proposition 4.4.
* `P67/logs/quotient45/finite_places_by_jfibers.log` — the original run of `P67/quotient45/finite_places_by_jfibers.m` (Proposition 4.6), which decomposes the fibres of the `j`-map of `Y_F2` above the five source and seven target `j`-invariants and writes `P67/data/quotient45/source_places_data.m` and `target_places{1..7}_jbase_data.m`. After the export (`NONIC67H3_FINITE_PLACES_ALL_COMPLETE`, at 2226 s) the original script went on with optional Riemann-Roch controls on three divisors (two gave dimension 0), and the run was killed with SIGTERM by the Codex CPU budget of 5400 s during the third; so the log ends without a closing marker. The data files had been written completely before. The committed script is the original without that control tail (the paper does not use it). It was rerun for this repository (`P67/logs/quotient45/finite_places_by_jfibers_rerun.log`, exit 0, 2171 s): its output agrees with this log up to timings and paths, and the nine data files it writes are byte-identical to the committed ones. Independently, `reemit_validated_data.m` (a complete run) re-validates all 132 place keys and the `σ`-permutations on the canonical model.
* `P73/logs/readonly_complete_checks.log` — the output of the eight independent replays for Proposition 4.8 (`P73/quotient19/verify_q_identities.py`, `verify_winding.py`, `verify_finite_keys.py`, `verify_canonical_ring_tensor_readonly.py`, `verify_multiplication_certificate_readonly.py`, `verify_ranks_m4ri.py`, `common/verify_FI_relations.py P73/data/FI_rows.txt` and `common/replay_rational_two.py 73`), each preceded by a `CHECK` line with the command. It was written by an inline Python driver typed into the Codex session (no script file), which also checked the hashes of the packet. Each replay can be run on its own with the commands above; `python3 P73/certify_73_readonly.py` runs the same eight replays and checks that they pass (`P73/logs/root_readonly.log`).
* `P79_P89/logs/P79/readonly_checks.log` and `P79_P89/logs/P89/readonly_checks.log` — the outputs of the independent replays of the two quotient computations of Proposition 4.9, written by an inline Python loop in the Codex session (not a script), which ran `verify_q_identities_complete.py`, `verify_winding.py`, `verify_finite_keys.py` (for 89 `verify_finite_keys_jv.py`), `verify_canonical_ring_tensor_readonly.py`, the multiplication-certificate replay and the M4RI rank check of `P79_P89/P79/quotient18/` (resp. `P79_P89/P89/quotient27/`), and for 79 also `common/verify_FI_relations.py` on `P79_P89/data/P79/FI_rows.txt`, and saved each command line followed by its output. They are kept because they are the only record of the full output of these replays. Each script can be run on its own (`python3 P79_P89/P79/quotient18/verify_winding.py`, ...), and `P79_P89/certify_79_readonly.py` and `certify_89_readonly.py` run all of them (`P79_P89/logs/P79/root_readonly.log`, `P79_P89/logs/P89/root_readonly.log`, which record only that each passed).
* `C133/logs/direct_sharp_model.log` is the log of the original producer run of `C133/direct_sharp_model.m` (which loads `direct_sharp_tail.m` and `direct_sharp_rr.m`; `magma -b`, exit code 0, 4737 CPU seconds). It computed the degree-20 model of Z over F_2, the Cartier and diamond matrices and the 20 Riemann-Roch kernels at the cusps, exported to `C133/data/fi3_data_sharp_direct.m`. `direct_sharp_tail.m` was edited twice after the run was launched, before Magma loaded it (the printed operator label and the exported image basis show that the edited version, the one committed here, ran). The formal-immersion test in this log uses the product of the two finite operators (cotangent image of dimension 8) and fails in 4 configurations; the proof uses the two operators separately. The exported data do not depend on the operator, and were re-analysed with the split operators by `replay_split_operators.m` and `replay_split_fi.py` (`replay_split_operators.log`, `replay_split_fi.log`: U = 22, all 58 configurations of rank 3). The whole computation was rerun for this repository with `C133/reproduce_FI3.m`, which uses the split operators from the outset and fails on any exception: `C133/logs/reproduce_FI3.log`, `C133/data/fi3_data_reproduced.m`, replayed in `replay_split_fi_reproduced.log`; these are the primary evidence.
* `C38/logs/N38_p3_quotient_D9.log` and `C38/logs/N38_p3_quotient_D9_resume.log` — the original degree-9 Hecke sieve at 3 on `X_1(38)` (Proposition 6.1), `common/hecke_sieve_quotient_D9.m` with `N:=38 BaseNs:=19 p:=3 Qs:=5,7,11,13,17,19 D:=9 M:=1 MemGB:=12`. The first run was started under `timeout 1500` and was stopped by the time limit during the type (6,3), after it had completed the types (3), (5), (6), (3,3), (7), (8), (5,3), (9) (399 tests); the second run (`FromDegree:=9 MaxPart:=6`, under `timeout 500`) completed the types (6,3) and (3,3,3) (39 tests) and ended normally. The original proof took each type from the run that completed it. The whole sieve was rerun without a time limit for this repository, with the same command (`C38/logs/N38_p3_quotient_D9_rerun.log`, 455 CPU seconds); it is the primary log, it agrees line for line (up to timings) with the two original runs on the types that each of them completed, and `C38/audit_N38_D9.py` audits it. `python3 C38/audit_N38_D9.py N38_p3_quotient_D9.log N38_p3_quotient_D9_resume.log` repeats the original audit of the two archived logs (it writes `C38/data/N38_D9_audit.json`).
* `C46_C50/logs/degree8/N46_p3_single_completed.log` — inherited from the degree-8 record (the same file is archived in the octic repository): the first 282 lines of the degree-8 Hecke sieve at 3 on `X_1(46)` (`N:=46 p:=3 D:=8 M:=1 Qs:=5,7,11,13,17,19 MemGB:=4`, `common/degree8/hecke_sieve_smart.m`), up to the end of its type (8), followed by the line `SINGLE_BRANCH_CHECKPOINT_DONE ...`, which was written by hand when the log was cut and which no script prints; the run itself was stopped by hand during the type (4,4). It contains the single-place types (4)–(8) (109 tests, all killed), for which the original degree-9 proof cited it, and `C46_C50/audit_46_50_D9.py` (without `--rerun`) still reads it. The whole degree-9 sieve on `X_1(46)` was rerun for this repository (`C46_C50/logs/N46_p3_quotient_D9_rerun.log`, 2166 CPU seconds); it is the primary evidence for all 782 tests, and `python3 C46_C50/audit_46_50_D9.py --rerun` audits it. A complete rerun of the degree-8 sieve is in the octic repository (`C46/logs/N46_p3_smart_rerun.log`).
* `C58_C62/logs/C62_X131/known31/verify_known31.log` — the first run of `known31/verify_known31.py` (26 October 2026, 22:03, in a Codex tool session, not as a batch job). It was made with an earlier version of the script, which read the classification, the finite points, the geometry and van Hoeij's input from their original locations; a minute later the script was changed to read byte-identical local copies (the two lines `TOP=` and `INP=`, nothing else), and the root certificate reran the changed script in `--check` mode, which asserts that it reproduces `binding31.json` (`C58_C62/logs/C62_root_readonly.log`). Because no committed script produced this log, the computation was rerun for this repository with the repository's script; the rerun log `verify_known31_rerun.log` has the same three lines and is the one cited, and the rerun rewrote `binding31.json` byte for byte.
* `C35/logs/presentation_v2/replay_dual35.log` — the original log of the independent integer replay `C35/presentation_v2/replay_dual35.py --write-report` of the primary-character classification of `Z/35` (Proposition 6.4). It was written before the last edit of the script, so no committed script is known to have produced it. The read-only certificate of the original record reran the final script (without saving its output). The replay was rerun for this repository with the committed script (`C35/logs/presentation_v2/replay_dual35_rerun.log`); its output line is identical, and that log is the one cited.
* Degree-8 computation behind the inputs of Proposition 6.5 (the same files and explanation as in the octic repository): `C48/logs/degree8/probe_source_places.log`, `probe_source_places_5_7.log`, `source_octic_places.log`, `export_source_partition_octic_count_completion.log` — the source side of `Z/48` (Proposition 9.9 of the octic paper) was computed in one interactive Magma session, which ran `target_2_24_setup.m` (an earlier target setup, not kept), `probe_source_places.m` (places of degree 3, 4), `probe_source_places_5_7.m` (degrees 5–7), a probe of quotient maps that was interrupted, `source_octic_places.m` (the 48992 octic places), a first export of the diamond orbits that ran out of memory, a resumed export that was interrupted after the memory limit had been raised by hand, and finally `export_source_partition_octic_count_completion.m`, which completed the octic orbits (stopping after 38173 places, when the orbit union reached the Newton count 48992) and appended them to `C48/data/degree8/source_partition_inputs_complete.py`. These steps use variables of the session, so no single command regenerates the four logs. What the proof uses from them is checked independently: the place counts are the Newton counts printed in `C48/logs/degree8/clean_run/target_2_24_setup.log`, and `C48/degree8/replay_source_partitions.py` verifies with its own finite-field and group-law arithmetic that the exported points have exact order 48 and that their diamond orbits are disjoint and exhaust the counts (`C48/logs/degree8/source_partition_replay.log`).
* Degree-8 computation behind the inputs of Proposition 6.5 (the same files and explanation as in the octic repository): `C48/logs/degree8/clean_run/filter_lower_supports.log`, `C48/logs/degree8/clean_run/high_target_aut_orbits.log` — written in an interactive Magma session that loaded, in this order and without interruption, `C48/degree8/target_2_24_clean_setup.m` (which wrote `C48/logs/degree8/clean_run/target_2_24_setup.log`, reproducible on its own), `C48/degree8/initialize_clean_filter.m` (the proved class group of `Y`, the factor-base export and the low-support filter `filter_lower_supports_clean.m`) and `C48/degree8/prepare_high_target_aut_orbits.m`. The steps depend on the session state, and the class-group presentation is computed with random choices, so a rerun can produce different but equivalent presentations; the exported data (`C48/data/degree8/clean_run/`) and the replays `replay_low_class_filter.py`, `replay_high_target_aut_orbits.py`, `replay_proved_presentations.py` check the results independently. Starting `magma` and loading the three scripts in this order from the repository root repeats the session.
* Degree-8 computation behind the inputs of Proposition 6.5 (the same files and explanation as in the octic repository): `C48/logs/degree8/clean_run_v2/inverse_and_fastfb_v3.log`, `baseline_control_identities.log`, `fastfb_control_identities.log`, `high_shard0.log`–`high_shard7.log`, `high_shard0_identities.log`–`high_shard7_identities.log`, `low_factorbase_key_replay.log` — written in a second interactive session, started as `magma -b C48/degree8/run_high_setup_v2.m` (the target group again, `C48/degree8/initialize_high_clean_v2.m`, which also loads the earlier control `validate_inverse_and_fastfb_v2.m`), into which `C48/degree8/validate_inverse_and_fastfb_v3.m` (the corrected control, which rewrote the two identity logs), `C48/degree8/filter_high_target_clusters_fork_v2.m` (the 3507 high class tests, in eight processes forked from the session, each writing one shard log, its arithmetic and its identity log with the witnesses `*.witnesses.py` beside it), `C48/degree8/export_target_function_field_bindings.m` and `C48/degree8/recheck_low_via_factorbase_keys.m` were loaded by hand. The forked shards share the proved presentation of the session, and the witnesses depend on random choices, so the logs cannot be regenerated by one command. Every class test is replayed: `replay_high_class_filter.py` recomputes the filter from the exported arithmetic, and the eight standalone scripts `C48/degree8/clean_run_v2/high_witness_replay_v3_shard*.m` recheck all 3507 principal-ideal identities from the exported witnesses and factor-base geometry (their logs are reproducible).
* `C51/logs/N51_degree9_FI_replay.log` — the original run of `C51/N51_replay_degree9.py` (Proposition 7.2), which also wrote the replay JSON. The script was edited after this run (the log is from 11:34, the final script from 12:08), so the log and the original JSON were written by an earlier version, which is not preserved; the read-only replay with the final script (`C51/logs/N51_degree9_root_replay.log`) agreed with the stored JSON. For this repository the final script was rerun (`C51/logs/N51_degree9_FI_replay_rerun.log`); it wrote the committed `C51/data/N51_degree9_FI_replay.json`, which differs from the original JSON only in the elapsed time.
* `C51/logs/degree8/N51_prefix27_RR_control_root.log` — inherited from the degree-8 record (archived in the octic repository as well): a control of the `X_1(51)` export by direct Riemann–Roch (`C51/degree8/N51_prefix27_RR_control_root.m`), run under `timeout 900`. It computed the Cartier matrix and the differential-space kernels at the first cusp for the multiplicities 1 and 8 and was stopped by the time limit during the third kernel (cusp 15, multiplicity 1). Only the two completed kernels, written to `C51/data/degree8/N51_prefix27_RR_control_root_data.m`, are used: `common/degree8/compare_rr_to_completions.py` checks that they agree with the export (`C51/logs/degree8/N51_full_RR_crosscheck_root.log`). `magma -n -b C51/degree8/N51_prefix27_RR_control_root.m` without a time limit computes all three kernels.
* `C55/logs/degree8/large_levels/C55_cover_model_v4.log` — inherited from the degree-8 record (it is `C55/logs/C55_cover_model_v4.log` of octic_torsion): the first computation of the genus-17 model of `X_1(55)/⟨16⟩` over F_2 and of its degree-8 Cartier test, in an interactive Magma session: `C55_cover_model_v4.m` was loaded with `SetLogFile` on this file, then `C55_cartier_continue.m` was loaded by hand into the same session, which wrote `C55/data/degree8/large_levels/C55_cartier_kernels.m`. `C55/degree8/large_levels/C55_cartier_reproduce.m` is the concatenation of the two scripts, and its batch run (`C55_cartier_reproduce.log`) wrote a byte-identical export. Nothing in the nonic proof rests on this log: the degree-9 runs rebuild and verify the model from `C55_builtin_model.m`.
* `C69/logs/N69_cover_good_D9.log` — the original batch run of `C69/N69_cover_good_D9.m` (`magma -b N:=69`, exit status 0, 236 CPU seconds). The audit of this repository found two fail-open paths in the degree-9 branch of the script: a base place whose places above have degree not dividing 9 was skipped (`C69_D9_NO_LIFT`), and a base place inert in `Y` with odd multiplicity gave an empty list of lifts, so that the case passed with no test. Neither was triggered (the log has no `NO_LIFT` line, and the remaining case tested 2 lifts). For this repository both paths were turned into assertions, and the script was rerun; the rerun log `N69_cover_good_D9_rerun.log` is the one cited.
* `C77/logs/model_deck.log` is the original log of `C77/build_model_deck.m` (26 October 2026). The script first ended after the deck computation; while Magma was running it, the line loading `C77/rr9_continue.m` (the basis alignment, the new subspaces and the 3098 ranks) was appended to it, after an attempt to send that line through standard input had failed. Magma had then read 4096 of the final 5376 bytes and later executed the appended `load`, so the code that ran is the committed `build_model_deck.m` together with `rr9_continue.m`, but the log is not the run of an unchanged file. It is replaced by the clean rerun `C77/logs/model_deck_rerun.log` (job `magma -n -b C77/build_model_deck.m`), which agrees with it line by line up to timings and the paths in the `Loading` lines and regenerates `C77/data/fi9_data.json` byte for byte.
* `C57/logs/source85/replay_all_cusp_FI_sage.log` — the first run of the independent formal-immersion replay for Proposition 8.3 (all 796111 normalized cuspidal configurations, all of rank 9), which wrote `C57/data/source85/all_cusp_FI_sage_certificate.json`. Afterwards one assertion was added to the script (that the annihilating space is stable under the diamond operator ⟨5⟩), so the script here is not the one that produced this log. The read-only rerun with the final script, `C57/logs/source85/replay_all_cusp_FI_sage_readonly.log` (`python3 C57/source85/replay_all_cusp_FI_sage.py --readonly`, which recomputes everything and compares it with the certificate), gives the same output up to timings and is the log cited.
* `C57/logs/source85/assemble_RR_divisors_sage.log` — the first run of the assembly of the three divisors `B = A_5(σP−P)` of Proposition 8.3 (arrangement `[1,4,−5]`, positive degrees 42, 42, 88), which wrote the certificate `C57/data/source85/rr_divisors_sage_certificate.json`. The script was edited twice afterwards: the data files `rr_places_data.txt` and `rr_divisors_data.txt` were rewritten in Magma's `[* *]` list syntax (same content), and a `--readonly` mode was added. `C57/logs/source85/assemble_RR_divisors_sage_rerun.log`, a read-only run of the final script made for this repository, gives the same output up to timings and is the log cited.
* the original run of `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/C2x32/four_quotients.m` (Proposition 11.3, `Z/2 × Z/32`: the 107 cases and the four maps to `X_1(32)`), a batch run (`magma -b`, exit status 0, 615 s), which also wrote `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/data/C2x32/four_quotients_arithmetic.txt` and `four_quotients_survivors.m`. The script was rewritten in the packaging step, 13 minutes after the run; its printed output agrees with the log and it differs from the prepared reproduction script `four_quotients_reproduction.m` only in the output paths, but no hash of the version that ran exists. The computation was therefore repeated with the script as it stands here (`C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/logs/C2x32/four_quotients_rerun.log`, the log cited for Proposition 11.3); `replay_full.py` replays the exported arithmetic.
* `C2x36/logs/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/prefix_native.log` (with `prefix_RR_identity.log`, `C2x36/data/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/prefix_RR_identity.witnesses.py`, `prefix_arithmetic.txt`, `prefix_snapshot.json`, `retained_prefix_survivors.txt`) — the first run of the four-map `X_1(36)` test (Proposition 11.6) on all 2064 supports left by the map to `X_1(2,18)`, `magma -b filter_cyclic36_linear.m` (kept as `C2x36/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/prefix_source.m`). When the genus-9 quotient had reduced the 2064 supports to 267, the run was stopped with SIGSTOP after case 420 (state T at 3099.7 CPU seconds) and killed after the acceptance of the record (exit −15); it never completed. Its log, its Riemann–Roch identity log and its checkpoint (written by the script itself every 20 cases; cases 1–420, of which 67 are among the 267 genus-9 survivors, with one survivor, [1,8,1601]) were copied at case 420 under the names `prefix_*`; the witness file was closed by hand (the brackets the script writes at its normal end were appended). `tail.m` then treated the other 200 of the 267 supports in a complete run (`C2x36/logs/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/tail.log`, none passes), and `replay_cyclic_sage.py` replays the union of the two (`replay_cyclic_sage_complete.log`). So 66 of the exclusions and the identification of the last support rested on the stopped run. For this repository `tail.m` was rerun on all 267 supports in one fail-closed batch run (`C2x36/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/tail_all267.m`, `C2x36/logs/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/tail_all267.log`, replayed by `replay_cyclic_all267_sage.py`): it leaves exactly [1,8,1601], as the two original runs together; this rerun is the one Proposition 11.6 cites. Running `prefix_source.m` writes the complete run under the original names `filter_cyclic36_linear_arithmetic.txt` and `cyclic36_linear_RR_identity.log` and does not touch the archived files.
* `C2x36/logs/diagonal_quotient/tate_cusp_alignment_original.log` — the original log of `C2x36/diagonal_quotient/tate_cusp_alignment.py` (Lemma 11.5), written before the script was edited (the edit added the `--readonly` mode). The edited script's read-only control run in the record printed the identical text (`tate_readonly_control.log`, byte-identical, not kept separately), and the rerun of the current script made for this repository, `C2x36/logs/diagonal_quotient/tate_cusp_alignment.log`, is again identical and rewrites `tate_cusp_alignment.json` and `cusp_permutations.m` byte for byte.
* `C2x42/logs/stabilizer_filter_sage_original.log` — the original log of `C2x42/stabilizer_filter_sage.py` (the even-stabilizer filter of Proposition 11.9, 919 → 823 supports, of which 18 remain after the map to `X_1(21)`), written five minutes before a dead expression was removed from the script. The rerun of the current script made for this repository, `C2x42/logs/stabilizer_filter_sage.log`, is byte-identical to it and rewrites `C2x42/data/stabilizer_filter_sage.json` and `after_even_stabilizer_descriptors.txt` byte for byte.
* `C2x42/logs/genus5_quotient/replay_diagonal_resolvent_sage_original.log` — the original log of `C2x42/genus5_quotient/replay_diagonal_resolvent_sage.py` (the cubic resolvent of the diagonal quotient `Z`, Lemma 11.8 and Proposition 11.9), written before the script was edited (a `--readonly` mode was added). The rerun of the current script made for this repository, `C2x42/logs/genus5_quotient/replay_diagonal_resolvent_sage.log`, is byte-identical to it and rewrites `diagonal_resolvent_sage_certificate.json` byte for byte.
* `C2x26/logs/class_2_26_D9.log` — the first degree-9 run of the class test on `X_1(2,26)` at 3 (`C2x26/class_2_26_D9.m`, the degree-8 script `C2x26/degree8/class_2_26_D8.m` extended by the degree-9 places and the cubic-cusp complements in degree 6). It was started as `timeout 2700 magma -b C2x26/class_2_26_D9.m` and killed at the wall-time limit (exit 124) after the degree-5..8 branch and 116 of the 388 degree-9 diamond classes; its last line is a CPU time. Nothing in it is used: the degree-5..8 branch was rerun for this repository as one fail-closed batch run, `C2x26/class_2_26_D9_lower8.m` (`C2x26/logs/class_2_26_D9_lower8.log`, exit 0, `tested=239 remaining=0`), and the degree-9 places are treated by `C2x26/N26_D9_threequotients.m` and `C2x26/class_last2.m` (Proposition 11.10).
* `C2x26/logs/N26_lower/fresh_D9_completed_lower8_prefix.log` — the first 737 lines of the killed run `C2x26/logs/class_2_26_D9.log`, cut out by Codex: the setup, the class group and the complete degree-5..8 branch (239 classes, none in `H`). The Codex proof cited this prefix for the 239 lower-degree classes; here they are covered by the complete degree-8 run `C2x26/degree8/class_2_26_D8.m` (replayed in degree 9 by `C2x26/N26_lower/replay_lower8_D9.py`) and by the clean degree-9 rerun `C2x26/class_2_26_D9_lower8.m`. `python3 C2x26/N26_lower/verify_fresh_lower8_prefix.py` (without argument) still checks that the prefix has the same 239 classes as the degree-8 export; with the rerun log as argument it is the job of `C2x26/logs/N26_lower/verify_lower8_classes.log`.

## Which script proves what

Numbering refers to the manuscript (draft of 27 October 2026), and the rows follow its order.
"[N8]" is the octic paper.

| **Proposition 12.1 (Z/29, Z/31, Z/32, Z/33, Z/34, Z/39, Z/40, Z/42).** The 19 points of degree 9 on `X_1(N)`, `N ≥ 29`, of van Hoeij's `LowDegreePlaces` (levels 29, 31, 32 (eight points), 33, 34 (four), 39, 40, 42 (two)); the eight of the proposition are the entries 1, 2, 3, 11, 12, 16, 17, 18. | **Proposition 12.1 (Z/2 × Z/26: the curve E_0 over K_0).** The polynomial defining `K_0` is irreducible modulo 3; `(0,0)` and `(b,b)` have orders 2 and 26 and `(0,0) ≠ 13(b,b)`; `E_0` has good reduction at the prime above 3, with 19604 points over `F_{3^9}`, and at the four primes above 7, of residue degrees 2, 2, 2, 3, with 52, 52, 52, 364 points; the reductions are recounted in Sage, and the Magma check was repeated from a fresh directory in the original record | `C2x26/explicit/verify_native.m`; `C2x26/explicit/reproduce.py` (with `verify_native_reproduction.m`); replay `C2x26/explicit/replay_sage.py` | `C2x26/logs/explicit/verify_native.log`, `C2x26/logs/explicit/fresh_reproduction/native.log`, `C2x26/logs/explicit/replay_sage.log`; `C2x26/data/explicit/example.txt`, `native_arithmetic.txt`, `independent_certificate.json` |
| **Proposition 12.1 (additional: the 2-isogenous curves; Codex Corollary 3.128, not used in the paper).** For the 15 entries of even level `N`, the quotient by the point of order 2 of `⟨(0,0)⟩` has torsion `Z/N` (`N = 34, 42`) or `Z/2 × Z/(N/2)` (`N = 32, 40`), by the same reductions | `realizations/verify_nonic_two_isogenies.m` | `realizations/logs/verify_nonic_two_isogenies.log` |
| **Proposition 12.1 (additional: a second proof that Z/2 × Z/26 occurs; Codex Lemma 3.46 and Proposition 3.47, not used in the paper).** On `X_1(2,26)` (18 rational cusps and three orbits of 6 cusps, labelled by Tate curves), rational cuspidal divisors `A`, `B`, `C` stable under the level automorphism `h` of order 3 satisfy `A ~ C` (an explicit function at 3 and at 7), `h^0(A) = 2` and `h^0(A - B) = 1`; the unique divisor in `\|A - B\|` reduces at 3 to one place of degree 9 and at 7 to places of degree 2, 2, 2, 3, where the curves have 52, 52, 52, 364 points; cusp labels, `h`-cycles and point counts are replayed in Python, and the Magma run was repeated from a fresh directory in the original record | `C2x26/occurrence/finite_geometric_certificate.m`; `C2x26/occurrence/reproduce.py` (with `finite_geometric_reproduction.m`); replays `C2x26/occurrence/tate_cusp_replay.py`, `C2x26/occurrence/groups_replay.py` (with `common/finite_arithmetic_char3.py`, `C2x30/finite_arithmetic.py`) | `C2x26/logs/occurrence/finite_geometric_certificate.log`, `reproduce.log`, `reproduced_run/native.log`, `tate_cusp_replay.log`, `groups_replay.log`; `C2x26/data/occurrence/` |
| **Proposition 12.1 (how E_0 was found; discovery, not part of any proof).** A Miller function on `X_1(2,26)` with divisor `A - C` (built in characteristic 0 and checked at 3 and 7) takes the value -1/4 on the branch of the cusp in `B`; eliminating it against the plane model `mdmagma/models_X1_m_n/X1_2_26.txt` gives a resultant with one factor of degree 9, the polynomial of `K_0`, and the curve `E_0` | `C2x26/discovery/miller_pencil_function.m` (and `miller_pencil_function_charzero_driver.m`), `exact_branch_value.m`, `extract_explicit_field.m` | `C2x26/logs/discovery/*.log`; `C2x26/data/discovery/` |
| **Remark after Proposition 12.1 (the Z/42 example with j = -140625/8).** Over the cubic field of `check_cubic21_two.m` the curve `E(b,c)` of a cubic point of `X_1(21)` has a point of order 21 and `j = -140625/8`; its 2-division polynomial is irreducible with non-square discriminant, so it gains a point of order 2 over a cubic extension, a nonic field | `realizations/check_cubic21_two.m` | `realizations/logs/check_cubic21_two.log` |

## Independent recomputations

The folder `independent/` holds computations made during the review of the original record with
code written independently of it (Magma, PARI/GP and Python/Sage; none of them imports a script of
the record). Each task folder holds its scripts and logs; `independent/SUMMARY.md` summarizes the
results. All of them agree with the claims of the paper.

Their inputs are the list of groups of Najman–Varivoda, van Hoeij's `LowDegreePlaces`
(`independent/task2/vh_deg9.m` holds the entries of degree 9, extracted by
`independent/task2/extract_vh.py`), the published papers they cite, and the LMFDB. Not recomputed
here: the Hecke sieves, the class-group computations, the canonical-ring sieves and the Cartier and
Riemann–Roch tests of the individual cases (most of these are replayed by the replays listed in the
table above), and the ranks at the levels 841, 899 and 961 and of the 31 levels of Table 5.

| Statement | Script | Log |
|---|---|---|
| **Lemma 3.1.** Φ^∞(9) as in Najman–Varivoda, Theorem 1.1(3), has 42 elements; the 51 groups of Theorem 1.1 are closed under subgroups; the minimal groups outside them with prime divisors at most 89 are exactly the 94 groups of the proof (87 cyclic, 7 noncyclic), the largest cyclic one of order 961 | `independent/task1/minimal_obstructions.py` | `independent/task1/minimal_obstructions.log` |
| **Proposition 12.1 and Theorem 1.3.** For the 19 points of degree 9 of van Hoeij on `X_1(N)`, N = 29, 31, 32, 33, 34, 39, 40, 42, and the curve with Z/2 × Z/26: the torsion subgroup over the nonic field, computed three ways (Magma's `TorsionSubgroup`, PARI's `elltors`, and the gcd of the reductions at at least 40 primes), is the claimed group in all 20 cases; the fields, their LMFDB labels and Galois groups, the degree of `Q(j)`, no CM, and the (p, f, h) reduction data of the paper | `independent/task2/check_entry.m` (`Idx:=1..20`), `independent/task2/pari/check_pari.gp`, `independent/task2/lmfdb_lookup.py` | `independent/task2/entry_01.log` … `entry_20.log`, `independent/task2/pari/check_pari.log`, `independent/task2/lmfdb_lookup.log` |
| **Ranks (Sections 4–11).** For the 45 levels N of `J_1(N)` and the 10 levels of `J_1(2,2n)` used in the paper (55 in all): the decomposition of the space of cusp forms into Galois orbits of newforms, and whether the winding element has a nonzero projection to each (L(f,1) ≠ 0); the weighted dimensions add up to the genus at every level. `J_1(N)` and `J_1(2,2n)` have rank 0 exactly where the paper says so, and at the other levels the characters of the factors outside the winding quotient are trivial on the diamond operators the paper uses | `independent/task3/rankcheck.m` (`Kind:=J1` or `J22`, `N:=...`) | `independent/task3/logs/*.log` |
| **Counts of closed points over finite fields (Sections 4–11).** All the numbers of noncuspidal closed points of degree at most 9 on `X_1(N)` and `X_1(2,2n)` over F_2, F_3 and F_5 quoted in the paper (47 lists), and the absence of points of order 77, 95, 97, 99, 101 over F_{2^e}, e ≤ 9, from a weighted enumeration of all elliptic curves over F_{p^e}, p ≤ 5, e ≤ 9 | `independent/task4/hist.gp` (driven by `independent/task4/drive.sh`), `independent/task4/counts.py`, `compare_claims.py`, `details.py` | `independent/task4/compare_claims.log`, `independent/task4/details.log` |
| **Lemma 2.11.** The torsion groups of CM elliptic curves over nonic fields are the 11 groups Z/1, Z/2, Z/3, Z/4, Z/6, Z/9, Z/14, Z/18, Z/19, Z/27, Z/2 × Z/2 (Clark–Corn–Rice–Stankewicz), rederived from the results of Bourdon and Pollack | `independent/task5/bp_strong_odd_degree.py` | `independent/task5/bp_strong_odd_degree.log` |

The histograms of task 4 are not included (344 MB). To regenerate them, run, from the folder
`independent/task4`, `./drive.sh p e K k` for every line `p e K k` of `jobs.txt` (33 chunks; they
write `hist/h_p_e_kofK.txt`, `hist/d_p_e_kofK.txt` and a log, with PARI/GP, and can run in
parallel, e.g. `parallel --colsep ' ' ./drive.sh {1} {2} {3} {4} :::: jobs.txt`), and then
`python3 compare_claims.py > compare_claims.log` and `python3 details.py > details.log`; this is why
these two logs are archive-only in `jobs.txt`. `independent/task2/lmfdb_lookup.py` queries the
public LMFDB mirror and needs network access. `independent/task2/pari/check_pari.gp` is run with
PARI/GP (`cd independent/task2/pari && gp -q check_pari.gp`); its log is archive-only in
`jobs.txt` because `verify_all.sh` runs only Magma and Python jobs.

## Reruns made for this repository

The reviews of the original record found that some logs on which a proof relies came from runs
that were killed or cut by a time limit, from a log cut by hand out of a stopped run, from a run
whose time-limit monitor had been stopped by hand, or from a script that was edited during or after
its run. For this repository these computations were run again as single fail-closed batch jobs
(`magma -n -b`, from the repository root, with the scripts as they stand here, standard input at
end of file, exit status and closing marker checked) on 27 October 2026, on the same machine and
with the same Magma version. Python and Sage replays whose saved log predates the last version of
the script, and a few Python steps of the record that wrote data but no log, were run in the same
way; their new logs are listed here too. The new logs are the ones cited in
[Which script proves what](#which-script-proves-what); the original logs are kept as archive-only
logs. Every rerun completed without errors and reproduced the result of the original computation.

| Proposition 4.6 (Z/67): the decomposition of the 12 fibres of the j-map of Y = X_1(67)/H (genus 45) over F_2, without the optional Riemann-Roch controls of the original script | magma -n -b P67/quotient45/finite_places_by_jfibers.m (2171 s) → `P67/logs/quotient45/finite_places_by_jfibers_rerun.log` | exit 0, NONIC67H3_FINITE_PLACES_ALL_COMPLETE: 55 source points (5 σ-orbits of 11) and 7 target fibres of 11 points; same output as the original up to timings and paths; source_places_data.m and target_places{1..7}_jbase_data.m byte-identical to the committed files | P67/logs/quotient45/finite_places_by_jfibers.log (killed with exit -15 by a CPU budget during the RR-control tail; archived) |
| `Z/69`: the degree-9 tests on the cover of `X_1(69)` (Proposition 7.6) | `C69/N69_cover_good_D9.m` (`magma -n -b N:=69`), after two fail-open skips of the script were turned into assertions (see the notes on `C69`) → `C69/logs/N69_cover_good_D9_rerun.log` (158 s) | `C69_D9_GOOD_DONE nc7_cases=3 nc7_remaining=0 nc9_cases=2 nc9_tests=2 nc9_remaining=0 complete=true`; the same output as the original run up to timings | `C69/logs/N69_cover_good_D9.log`, the original run of the version with the two skips (neither was triggered) |
| The level `N` = 115, 161, 253, 299, 391, 437 of the global method (Proposition 5.1) | `common/winding_probe.m` (`magma -n -b N:=n`) → `large_levels/logs/inputs/winding{115,161,253,299,391,437}.log` (91 s in all) | the same winding rows and final lines as the logs copied into the record from the septic computations; `large_levels/build_large_certificate.py` rerun on the new logs writes the same certificate, and the logs of `certify_global_packet.py` and `certify_C115_q2_packet.py` are unchanged | copies of logs of the septic record, made by a version of the winding script that the record does not contain (not included) |

## The archived data

`common/data/` holds the files shared by several cases:

* `ledger.json` — the 9 realizations and 94 exclusions of the accepted proof ledger of the
  original record (`certified_results.json`), reduced to the groups, with the folder of this
  repository that holds each proof, and the prime bound and the published infinite families; read
  by `common/candidates.py` and the structural audit;
* `evidence_sha256.tsv` — every file the ledger lists as evidence, with the SHA-256 the ledger
  records and the path of the file here if it is kept byte for byte (logs and data, and the
  scripts whose paths did not change); the tests check these hashes;
* `inputs/LowDegreePlaces` — Mark van Hoeij's list of low-degree points on `X_1(N)`, from which the
  nonic points of the realizations are taken (`inputs/vanhoeij_nonic_input.{json,m}` hold the
  entries of degree 9, `inputs/vanhoeij_octic_input.m` those of degree 8 used for `X_1(33)` and
  `X_1(39)`); `C35/gonality` — his table of gonalities of `X_1(N)`;
* `lmfdb_newforms_cache.json` — the LMFDB records of the weight 2 newforms used by the global method;
* `dcm_list_all_min_orders_X1_10k.m` — the least degrees of CM points on `X_1(N)`, `N ≤ 10000`, from
  [least-cm-degree](https://github.com/fsaia/least-cm-degree) (Clark, Genao, Pollack, Saia), with
  its `least_cm_degree_README.md`;
* `candidates.json`, `structural_completeness_complete_20261027.json` — the outputs of the two
  scripts of Lemma 3.1.

The `data/` folder of each case holds the exported certificates and matrices that the replays
read, the fixed inputs of the sharded computations, the survivor lists and class arithmetic, and
the records written by the audits and replays.

Not included: the proof fragments of the original record (`.tex`; the paper replaces them),
copies of published papers and web pages, Magma's signature caches, the Codex prose (reviews,
readme files, status notes), the runner bookkeeping of the record (process status files, exit
records, watchdog logs and scripts), the scripts that only hashed evidence files and wrote
manifests or appended to the proof ledger, with the hash manifests they wrote, the copies of
mdmagma and of the GL2 package (now submodules), exact duplicates (merged into one file;
`original_names.tsv` lists every original name), and superseded, failed, killed or paused runs
that no statement relies on. The build report lists every omitted file and the reason.

## Provenance of the committed logs

The computations were run in the working folders of the original record, with paths relative to
its root (`work/degree9/...`) or absolute. For this repository the files were moved into the
present layout and every path in the scripts was rewritten accordingly. Apart from paths, the
Magma scripts are identical to the versions that ran, with these exceptions:

* Magma's `Write` and `PrintFile` append to an existing file. So that a rerun replaces the
  exported data instead of appending to the committed copy, the first write to each exported file
  now has the option `Overwrite:=true` (or the script truncates the file first). The original runs
  wrote to new files, so this does not change their output.
* A few scripts were given a parameter in place of a hand-edited constant, or a final assertion
  (noted below).

The Python audits and replays were adapted to the layout: they read and write the paths here.
Their checks of the SHA-256 hashes of scripts whose paths changed, and their reads of the proof
fragments, of copies of papers and of the original proof ledger, were removed, since the git
history and `common/data/evidence_sha256.tsv` now bind the files together. The logs are those of
the original runs, byte for byte (`common/data/evidence_sha256.tsv` records their SHA-256 in the
original ledger); the only new logs are those of the reruns described above. A rerun of an
adapted script can therefore print other paths or a shorter list of hashed files than its
committed log.

For this repository, every Python job and every Magma job whose recorded CPU time is under five
minutes (677 jobs) was rerun in a copy of the repository, in addition to the reruns
described above. All of them reproduced their committed logs up to the differences listed under
[Reproducing the computations](#reproducing-the-computations) (24 of them only when rerun on the
committed data, see there); the details are in the build notes. The pass found one error of the
adaptation for this repository, which is corrected: the first write of
`C77/degree8/C77_deck_reproduce.m` lacked `Overwrite:=true`.

Several computations were run twice in the original record, the second time by the reviewing
agent (the files called `..._root...`, `..._readonly...` or `fresh_...`). Where the two logs are
byte-identical, one of them is kept; otherwise both are kept, each with its own job.

Notes on the individual cases:

* `common`: `common/candidates.py` and `common/structural_completeness_complete_20261027.py` read the groups of the ledger from `common/data/ledger.json` (derived from `certified_results.json` of the record) instead of the ledger itself; their checks of the SHA-256 of the evidence files (and, for the structural audit, of 23 files of the earlier-degree records) were replaced by a check of the files kept here byte for byte against `common/data/evidence_sha256.tsv` (the structural audit prints `EVIDENCE_HASHES_PASS kept total`). The record has no log of the final run of either script (its `candidates.log` is from an earlier state of the ledger, with 16 obstructions still open); the logs here are from runs made for this repository, and their outputs agree with those of the record (`candidates.json`, `candidates.txt` identical; the structural JSON identical apart from the hash fields).
* `prime_bound`: `prime_bound/verify_prime_bound.py` originally parsed Table 2 of Derickx–Stoll (arXiv:2505.14109v1) from a saved copy of the arXiv HTML page, which is not distributed. It now reads the LaTeX source of that table (the `alttext` of the HTML element `S5.T2`), extracted verbatim into `prime_bound/data/derickx_stoll_table2.txt`, and still checks it against the table `TABLE2` of the authors' `strange_primes.magma` (`prime_bound/strange_primes_original.magma`). It no longer imports BeautifulSoup; it needs SymPy. Its other paths were changed to `prime_bound/data/` and `prime_bound/logs/`; its log and its JSON output are unchanged.
* `prime_bound`: Third-party code (GNU GPL v3): `prime_bound/DKSS_original.magma` and `prime_bound/strange_primes_original.magma` are unmodified copies of `DKSS/DKSS.magma` (Derickx, Kamienny, Stein, Stoll) and `PrimeTorsion_Asymptotics/strange_primes.magma` (Derickx, Stoll) of https://github.com/MichaelStollBayreuth/Verification (commit 7e60ef8); `prime_bound/DKSS_LICENSE` is the LICENSE of that repository. `DKSS_original.magma` is not run here (it loads `X1_p.magma`, not included); it is the source of the functions copied into the scripts below. `strange_primes_original.magma` is only read, as data, by `prime_bound/verify_prime_bound.py`.
* `prime_bound`: GPL-derived scripts: lines 1–117 of `prime_bound/prime_FI_certificate.m`, `P37/allbad_37_export.m`, `P41_P47_P59/allbad_41_47_export.m`, `P41_P47_P59/allbad_59_export.m`, `P43_P53_P61/allbad_43_export.m`, `P43_P53_P61/p53_allbad_FI_export.m`, `common/allbad_61_67_71_export.m` (and of `P73/allbad_73_export.m`; `common/allbad_79_83_89_export.m` differs only in its memory limit) are identical and contain the functions `kamiennyH`, `genH` and `kamienny` copied verbatim from DKSS.magma; these files are therefore distributed under the GNU GPL v3, whose text is next to them (`prime_bound/DKSS_LICENSE`, `P37/DKSS_LICENSE`, `P41_P47_P59/DKSS_LICENSE`, `P43_P53_P61/DKSS_LICENSE`, `common/GPL3_LICENSE`).
* `prime_bound`: The Codex reviewer reran `prime_FI_certificate.m` and `verify_prime_bound.py` with copies (`root_*`) that differ only in the names of their output files; their logs and outputs are byte-identical to the committed ones, so only one copy of each is kept.
* `prime_bound`: `prime_bound/logs/verify_prime_bound.log` and `prime_bound/data/prime_bound89_verified.json` call the primes 37–89 "unresolved": this refers to the prime bound alone; they are excluded in Propositions 4.3–4.10.
* `prime_bound`: `common/finite_field_prime_orders.m` (in the record `prime_orders/finite_field_prime_orders.m`) is used by the prime bound and by most of the prime and odd-level cases, so it is in `common/`.
* `P41_P47_P59`: `common/prime_good_branches.m` (and `common/prime_good_branches_64GB.m`, `C49/prime_and_49_good_branches.m`) write the representatives of the diamond orbits to `RecordFile` (`*/data/**/input_records.m`), which the controls (`controls_driver_v2.m`) and, for 37, `P37/finite_nc9_survivors.m`, `P37/sextic_lifts_control_v2.m` and `P37/sextic_cusp3_export.m` read. Magma does not fix the choice of the representatives, so a rerun may write other representatives (with the same counts and results); rerun the readers together with it, or restore the committed records with `git checkout` first.
* `P41_P47_P59`: The logs of the controls begin with Magma's `Loading "..."` lines, which name the paths of the original record; a rerun prints the repository paths.
* `P41_P47_P59`: The Codex runner scripts `run_magma_owned.py` and `run_magma_mutable_cpu.py`, which started the original runs and wrote their status and exit records, are not included; `jobs.txt` gives the Magma commands they ran.
* `P43_P53_P61`: `common/rational_two_diamond.m` exported its matrices (`MatrixFile`) by appending with `Write`; its first write now has `Overwrite:=true`, so a rerun replaces the committed file instead of appending to it (`P37/data/rational_two_delta4_matrices.m`, `P43_P53_P61/data/P43/N43_matrices.m`, and the exports of P71 and P73).
* `P43_P53_P61`: `common/replay_rational_two.py` read the matrices from its own folder; it now reads `P43_P53_P61/data/P43/N43_matrices.m` and `P73/data/deck/N73_matrices.m` from the repository root. The level-43 files of the original folder `deck/` are in `P43_P53_P61/logs/P43/` and `P43_P53_P61/data/P43/`.
* `P43_P53_P61`: `P43_P53_P61/verify_p53_allbad.py` records its elapsed time in `P43_P53_P61/data/p53_allbad_verified.json` and in its log; a rerun changes only that value.
* `P43_P53_P61`: `P43_P53_P61/p53_finite_keys.m` and `P43_P53_P61/p53_Hecke_keys_control.m` choose their representatives with `Rep`, so a rerun may print other (equivalent) points and keys.
* `P43_P53_P61`: The paper's computation for 53 uses a = PrimitiveRoot(53) = 2 (`p53_primitive_root_control.log`); for 61, `common/prime_good_branches_64GB.m` uses a = 2^6 mod 61 = 3.
* `P37`: `P37/replay_sextic_cusp3.py` and `P37/replay_rational_two.py` read their data next to themselves; they now read them from `P37/data/`. Run with `--readonly`, `P37/replay_sextic_cusp3.py` (like `C49/replay_mixed9.py`) compares its result, including the SHA-256 of the exported expansions, with the committed JSON. A rerun of `P37/sextic_cusp3_export.m` may order the rational cusps differently and so write a different, equivalent export; in that case run the replay without `--readonly` (which rewrites the JSON) or restore the committed export.
* `P37`: `P37/finite_nc9_survivors.m`, `P37/sextic_lifts_control_v2.m` and `P37/sextic_cusp3_export.m` use the representatives in `P37/data/input_records.m` written by the job of `P37/logs/good_branches_64GB.log` (orbits 3 and 4 of degree 9, orbit 1 of degree 6); see the note on `RecordFile`.
* `P67`: `P67/quotient45/finite_places_by_jfibers.m` is the original script without its last 25 lines (optional native Riemann-Roch controls on three divisors, during which the original run was killed); see the archive-only logs and the reruns.
* `P67`: `P67/quotient15/isogeny3_targets.m` was run in the folder of an earlier attempt with a quotient of genus 15 (not used); its export `isogeny3_target_data.m` was copied verbatim into `quotient45/` and is kept once, as `P67/data/quotient15/isogeny3_target_data.m`, which the `quotient45` scripts read.
* `P67`: P67/P71/P73: `canonical_change_basis_certified.m` is written by `align_differentials_certified*.py` and `plane_projection_equation.m` by `plane_differential_export.m`; both are data (in `data/`). The Python scripts of the quotient folders originally found their inputs next to themselves; they now read and write `<case>/data/<quotient>/`.
* `P67`: `P67/data/quotient45/modular_jbase_residue_data.m` (the 125 moduli and norm residues read by `modular_jbase_residue_check.m`) was extracted from `modular_jbase_norm_verified.json` by a two-line inline Python command of the Codex session; no script file exists for it. `modular_jbase_norm.py` (without `--readonly`) writes `jbase_relation_modular_data.m` and the JSON.
* `P67`: P67/P71/P73: the read-only certifiers `certify_67_readonly.py`, `certify_71_readonly.py`, `certify_73_readonly.py`, `certify_special9_readonly.py` and `certify_special7_readonly.py` were adapted: their checks of the Codex packet manifests (SHA-256 of every file, including scripts whose paths were rewritten), of `.tex` fragments and of Codex exit records were removed, and the corresponding keys (`files`, `manifest_sha256`, `selected_native_exits_zero`, `native_exits_zero`, `source_partial_files`) were dropped from their closing JSON line. The replays they run and their checks of logs and data are unchanged, so their `CHECK_PASS` lines are as in the committed logs.
* `P67`: P67/P71/P73: some printed flags are constants of the scripts rather than computed values (`both_eigenlines_if_applicable=true`, `infinity_checked=true`, `Frobenius_line_count11=true`); `P73/data/quotient19/canonical_formal_source_jets_v2_verified.json` says `"all_quadric_jet_equations_verified": 300`, a constant, while the script checks all 136 quadrics (for P71 the constant 276 and for P67 903 equal the real counts). The underlying assertions are in the code.
* `P67`: P67/P71/P73: rerunning the Python builders and sieves rewrites their JSON outputs (elapsed times) and the gzip files of matrices (the gzip header contains a time stamp); the matrices themselves are deterministic (fixed random seed).
* `P67`: `common/finite_remaining_census.m` (Table 3 for p = 61, 67, 71, 79, 83, 89) is cited by Propositions 4.4, 4.6, 4.7, 4.9 and 4.10.
* `P71`: `P71/logs/FI_relation_replay.log` is the relation-kernel replay (`common/verify_FI_relations.py`); an earlier prefix replay of the same rows (`P71/FI_replay.log` in the record) is not in the ledger and not included.
* `P73`: `jmap_q_export_v2.log`, `plane_differential_export_v2.log` and `finish_plane_model_certified_v2.log` are the second runs, after their inputs existed; the first attempts (`jmap_q_export.m` failed, the other two ran before their inputs were final) are not in the ledger and not included. `plane_differential_export.m` and `finish_plane_model_certified.m` were not changed between the runs.
* `P73`: `P73/allbad_73_export.m` contains the functions `kamiennyH`, `genH` and `kamienny` (lines 5-113) of the Magma code of Derickx, Kamienny, Stein and Stoll verbatim (GPL v3; `P73/DKSS_LICENSE` is a copy of the licence), followed by the export of the rows for p = 73.
* `P73`: `P73/allbad_73_export.m` writes `P73/data/FI_rows.txt`; `P73/data/FI_verified.json` was written by `python3 common/verify_FI_relations.py P73/data/FI_rows.txt P73/data/FI_verified.json` (transcript 2026-10-26T11:17Z, no log), and the committed log by the same command without the JSON argument.
* `P79_P89`: the Python scripts of `P79/quotient18/` and `P89/quotient27/` located their data files next to themselves; they are edited to read and write `P79_P89/data/P79/quotient18/` and `P79_P89/data/P89/quotient27/`. `canonical_change_basis_certified.m` (written by `align_differentials_certified.py`) and `plane_projection_equation.m` (written by `plane_differential_export*.m`) are data and moved to `data/`.
* `P79_P89`: P79_P89, Z/79: the plane model was computed twice; the first run (`finish_plane_model_certified.m`, not included) printed labels copied from the 89 script (`AF_degree=25`, `all300quadrics`), and the corrected rerun `finish_plane_model_reviewed.m` wrote a byte-identical model. Only the rerun is kept; `verify_finite_keys.py`, which read the first run's output file `plane_model_certified_data.m`, is edited to read `plane_model_reviewed_data.m` (same content).
* `P79_P89`: P79_P89, Z/89: two pipelines wrote byte-identical files: `plane_differential_data.m`/`plane_differential_certified_data.m`, `canonical_change_basis.m`/`canonical_change_basis_certified.m`, `plane_model_data.m`/`plane_model_certified_data.m`. Each pair is one file here, under the `_certified` name; it is written by the kept producers `plane_differential_export_certified.m`, `align_differentials_certified.py` and (both) `finish_plane_model_certified.m` and `finish_plane_model_v2.m`; the latter also checks natively that `σ` fixes `j`. The first producers of the uncertified copies (`plane_differential_export.m`, which read the output of a failed `j`-map probe, and `align_differentials.py`) are not included; `certify_89_readonly.py` no longer compares the merged pairs.
* `P79_P89`: `certify_79_readonly.py` and `certify_89_readonly.py` are adapted: paths of this repository; the SHA-256 checks of the files listed in the original packet manifests (`data/P79/manifest.json`, `data/P89/manifest.json`, still read for the file count they print) and the reads of the Codex exit records are removed; the printed counts `native_exits_zero` are constants of the original scripts.
* `P79_P89`: cosmetic: `build_formal_source_jets_v2.py` writes `"all_quadric_jet_equations_verified": 300` for 79 although it checks all 120 quadrics (hard-coded in the original). `common/allbad_79_83_89_export.m` also writes `P79_P89/data/P89/FI_rows.txt` (formal immersion rows for 89; not used by the paper).
* `P79_P89`: common/allbad_79_83_89_export.m contains the function `kamiennyH` of Derickx, Kamienny, Stein and Stoll (adapted from `prime_bound/DKSS_original.magma`; licence in `common/data/DKSS_LICENSE`); the output path of the rows is edited to the case folders.
* `P83`: the Python scripts of `canonical_units/` and `pole_bound_supplement/` are edited to read and write `P83/data/canonical_units/` and `P83/data/pole_bound_supplement/`; `verify_stage1_readonly.py` reads the model `mdmagma/models_X1_n/FFFc83.txt` of the mdmagma submodule. `certify_83_readonly.py` and `certify_supplement_readonly.py` are adapted: repository paths (the census logs are `common/logs/finite_remaining_census.log` and `common/logs/finite_remaining_primes_49.log`), and the SHA-256 checks of the original packets and the reads of Codex exit records are removed (the manifests are still read for the counts they print).
* `P83`: `P83/verify_FI_relations_v2.py` is `common/verify_FI_relations.py` with the initial value of the minimum prefix mass set to `None` (written by inline code in the Codex session); it produced `P83/logs/FI_relation_replay_v2.log` (relation kernel 0, so the minimum is `null`). `certify_83_readonly.py` runs the original version, which prints 100000 in that case.
* `P83`: the sieve `canonical_R1_sieve.py` (without `--readonly`) wrote `canonical_R1_sieve_verified.json` and `canonical_R1_matrices.json.gz`; the original run left no log. `build_R1_product_tensor.py`, `verify_stage1_readonly.py` and `verify_stage2_readonly.py` wrote their JSON outputs the same way. All are rerun in `--readonly` mode (or recomputed and compared) by `certify_83_readonly.py`.
* `P83`: not included: the withdrawn Fricke-convention supplement of 27 October 2026 (`P83/fricke_convention_supplement/`, `P83_root_fricke_readonly_20261027.log`, which asserted the wrong annihilator `T_3 - τ - 3`) and the paused native control `P83/good9_96GB` (no output). The proof uses the point-level annihilator `T_3 - 1 - 3<3>` (both arrangements are among the 410 tests).
* `large_levels`: the six winding logs `logs/inputs/winding{115,161,253,299,391,437}.log` were rerun for this repository with `common/winding_probe.m` (the script cited by the original ledger, `winding_probe_minimal.m`); the logs of the original record were copies of degree-7 logs produced by an earlier version of the probe that is not part of that record (it does not pass the character through `MinimalBaseRingCharacter`, so it prints larger coefficient degrees; the dimensions and winding dimensions agree). They keep the folder name `inputs/` because `data/inputs/degree8_large_certificate.json` refers to them by that name. `data/large_global_certificate.json` was regenerated by `build_large_certificate.py` from the new logs (only the coefficient degrees in the character orbits of the rows 161, 253, 299, 391, 437 changed). `logs/inputs/winding529_krylov_modp_v2.log` (with `data/inputs/winding529_krylov_matrix_v2.m`, `data/inputs/winding529_krylov_replay.json`) is the octic record's Krylov certificate for level 529, copied unchanged (the same files as in octic_torsion); its script is `winding_probe_krylov_modp_v2.m`.
* `large_levels`: `data/inputs/degree8_large_certificate.json` is the certificate of the octic large levels (`large_levels/data/large_certificate.json` of octic_torsion), read by `build_large_certificate.py` for the rows inherited from degree 8.
* `large_levels`: `build_large_certificate.py` writes `check_nonic_characters.m` (the exact character checks) and `data/large_global_certificate.json`; it reads the winding logs from `logs/` and leaves the levels 841, 899, 961 unfinished (their logs are in `logs/addendum*/`, they were completed afterwards as addenda).
* `large_levels`: `winding_probe_krylov.m`, `winding_probe_krylov_modp_v2.m` and `C115_image_even_orders_certified.m` export their files with Magma's `Write`, which appends; the writes now have `Overwrite:=true`. `winding_probe_krylov.m` writes the Krylov matrices of all levels to `large_levels/data/` (in the original record the matrices of 841, 899, 961 were also copied into the addendum folders); a rerun prints the new path in the `WINDING_KRYLOV_MATRIX` lines.

Winding accounting: the probe lists the constituents of `NewformDecomposition` of the cuspidal space; their dimensions add up to 217, 262, 216 instead of the genera 225, 265, 261 because the old constituents (levels 17, 13, 31) appear only once, with non-zero winding; the canonical-basis producers include all copies (with the degeneracy maps) and assert full rank.
