Its identical exports (`classes256.py`, `exceptional_fibres_data.m`, `exceptional_pencil_inputs.m`, `three_quotients.py`) are merged into `logs/run/`, so a rerun also writes them into `logs/reproduced_run/`, together with the generated stage wrappers `*_driver.m` and `completion.json` (not included). Changes to `reproduce_D9.py` besides paths: the repository root is the parent of its folder, Magma is called as `magma` from the PATH (option `--magma`), and an existing run directory is reused (`exist_ok=True`), so that the job rewrites the committed directory. `replay_D9.py` replays `C2x30/logs/run` by default (and `C2x30/logs/control`, the ordinary-ClassGroup control, whose `three_quotients.py` is byte-identical to that of the first run).
* `C2x30`: `C2x30`: `classes256_D9.m` and `exceptional_fibres_D9.m` were made by Codex from the degree-8 scripts (octic repository, `C2x30/src/`) by string substitution of the degree-dependent constants; `finite_arithmetic.py` is the arithmetic of `GF(7^n)` used by `replay_D9.py` (its docstring still describes the degree-8 data). `three_quotients_ordinary_control_D9.m` differs from `three_quotients_D9.m` only in using Magma's `ClassGroup` map instead of `MDClassGroupDirectRRCheckedV4FastFB`.
* `C2x30`: `common/native_group_generators.m` is the procedure `EvalDataGenerators` of Magma's package file `Ring/FldFun/classgroup.m` (V2.30-1, lines 699–740), copied verbatim apart from its name, the omitted profiling call and a header comment; `common/class_group_unflattened.m` likewise contains functions copied verbatim from that file (see the octic repository, `C40/data/diagnostics/class_group_unflattened.provenance.json`). The MIT license does not apply to this code. `common/class_group_direct_RR_checked_v4_fastfb.m`, `common/class_group_unflattened.m`, `common/native_group_generators.m` and `common/fast_diamond_hash_m2.m` are byte-identical to the files of the same names in the octic repository.
* `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: the Magma stages were started by small Python launchers (`run_*.py`, not included) that ran `magma -b SCRIPT` (for the enumeration `magma -b D:=d Limit:=0 Tag:=full<d> enumerate_direct.m`, `d = 4, …, 9`) from the root of the record under a CPU limit (14400 s; 7200 s for `filter_cyclic40.m`; 1800 s for `preflight.m`) and recorded exit status 0 for every stage; `jobs.txt` runs the same commands directly. The `X_1(40)` run was only reniced while it ran (6572 s wall time, 4419 s CPU).
* `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/data/C2x40/tate_cusps.json` and `cusp_degrees.txt` were written by an inline Python command calling `compute(2,40,3,9)` of `common/cusp_conditions_relative.py` (no script or log); `cusp_degrees.txt` is read by `preflight.m`, which checks `12(g-1) + 6·(sum of the cusp degrees) = 1152`. `python3 -c "import sys,json; sys.path.insert(0,'common'); from cusp_conditions_relative import compute; print(json.dumps(compute(2,40,3,9),indent=2))"` prints `tate_cusps.json` again. `cusp_conditions_relative.py` is also used by the replays of `C2x26` (its own `__main__` part writes the degree-8 table `cusp_conditions_relative.json` of the octic computations and is not used here).
* `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `enumerate_direct.m` exported `M2N40_D9_direct_full<d>_arithmetic.txt` by appending; its `Write` now has `Overwrite:=true`. The replays read `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/data/C2x32/`, `data/C2x34/`, `data/C2x40/` and write their certificates (`replay_full.json`, `independent_certificate.json`, `*_sage_certificate.json`) there; `C2x32/replay_full.py` imports `common/finite_arithmetic_char3.py`. `C2x32/reproduce.py` (with `four_quotients_reproduction.m`, which differs from `four_quotients.m` only in taking its output directory from `C232Out`) reruns the computation into a new directory and compares the exported arithmetic byte for byte; it now finds the repository root as the grandparent of its folder, calls `magma` from the PATH, and compares with `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/data/C2x32/four_quotients_arithmetic.txt`.
* `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/C2x34`: `four_quotients_cached.m` has four map branches but stops at the first failing map; all 239 cases fail at the first or second map (keeping `Q` or `P+Q`), so only these two enter the proof. It reads the source enumeration exported by `enumerate_source.m` (`data/C2x34/source_coverage.txt`, `source_frobenius.txt`). The failed first attempts (`four_quotients.m` and its `.v1_*`/`.v2_*` logs, an assertion failure in the orbit hashing, and an empty identity log) and the never-run `reproduce.py` with its two `*_reproduction.m` copies are not included.
* `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/C2x40`: superseded failed attempts in the record (`*_wrong_genus_*`, `*_scope_error_*`: assertions on a wrong genus and a Magma scope error), the unused `portable_proven_picard/` import, the stabiliser exploration `stabilizer_filter_sage.*` and two stale `.tex` notes were never part of the evidence and are not included. `filter_cyclic40.m` prepares four maps but every case fails the first (forgetful) map.
* `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: the class maps on `X_1(34)`, `X_1(2,20)` and `X_1(40)` use `MDClassGroupDirectRRCheckedV4FastFB` (`common/class_group_direct_RR_checked_v4_fastfb.m`), which checks every class identity in the script and writes the witnesses next to the `*_RR_identity.log` files; there is no separate rerun with Magma's ordinary `ClassGroup` map for these curves (unlike `C2x30`, where the control agrees byte for byte). `deps/fast_full_aut_hash_m2_32.m` is byte-identical to `C2x32/fast_full_aut_hash_m2_32.m` of the octic repository.
* `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40`: `C2x32_C2x34_C2x40/C2x40`: reruns reproduce the logs of `preflight.m`, `enumerate_direct.m` and the two `X_1(2,20)` filters up to timings and paths, but not all exported data byte for byte: `enumerate_direct.m` can list the 22 records of degree 8 in another order, and the `X_1(2,20)` filters pick their base cusp with `Rep` of a set, which can change between runs, so the class vectors in `filter_quotient20_*_arithmetic.txt` and the `*_RR_identity` logs change. The numbers of cases and survivors (234, 64, 27) do not change, and the three Sage replays pass on the regenerated exports as well (checked for this repository).
* `C2x36`: The X_1(36) step of Proposition 11.6 rests on the rerun `C2x36/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/tail_all267.m` (made for this repository; see Reruns and Archive-only logs). The two original runs (the stopped `prefix_source.m` = `filter_cyclic36_linear.m` and the complete 200-case `tail.m`) are kept; `tail.m` and `replay_cyclic_sage.py` have ordinary jobs (`replay_cyclic_sage.py` reads the archived checkpoint `prefix_arithmetic.txt`).
* `C2x36`: `tail.m` was derived from `filter_cyclic36_linear.m` (`prefix_source.m`) by changing only the output paths, the closing marker and the list of supports (`C2x36/data/cyclic_tail_after_genus9/tail_descriptors.txt`, the 200 genus-9 survivors not covered by the stopped run); `tail_descriptors.txt`, `retained_prefix_survivors.txt` and `prefix_snapshot.json` were written by inline Python of the Codex session that also stopped the first run.
* `C2x36`: `quotient_filter.m` recomputes the Hecke data of `proof_generator.m` before its filter; its exports `C2x36/data/diagonal_quotient/quotient_filter_hom_arithmetic.txt` and `quotient_filter_first_control_arithmetic.txt` are byte-identical to `proof_generator_hom_arithmetic.txt` and `proof_generator_kernel_arithmetic.txt` and are kept under their own names, since each script writes its own file.
* `C2x36`: The direct enumeration `enumerate_direct.m` appended to its export; it now overwrites it (`Overwrite:=true`). It lists its records in an order Magma does not fix, and the later filters use the record indices, so rerun the enumeration only together with the filters, or restore the committed `C2x36/data/M2N36_D9_direct_full*_arithmetic.txt` first.
* `C2x36`: The Python launchers `C2x36/run_enumeration.py`, `run_quotient18_linear.py`, `cyclic_tail_after_genus9/run_tail.py`, `diagonal_quotient/run_proof_generator.py`, `run_quotient_filter.py` and `orbit_section_criterion/run_kummer_certified.py` are the original supervisors (CPU limit, `magma -b`, exit record); they now call `magma -n -b` from `PATH` and write the logs into `C2x36/logs/`; their exit records (`*_completion.json`) are not committed. `jobs.txt` runs the same Magma commands directly.
* `C2x36`: The Sage replays read and write their data in `C2x36/data/...` (only the path anchors were changed); run without `--readonly` they rewrite the committed certificates, with `--readonly` they assert that the recomputed certificate equals the committed one.
* `C2x36`: `check_kummer_certified.m` was rerun after acceptance in the record (`noncyclic/C236_kummer_replay_20261026/`, a fresh `magma -b` run with other output names): all 13 exports were byte-identical to the committed ones; that folder is not included.
* `C2x42`: `genus5_quotient/replay_genus5_sage.py` reads the exports of the first run of the `A_11` audit on `Y`, `C2x42/genus5_quotient/audit_hecke/audit.m` (a clean `magma -b` run, identical to `audit_hecke_v2/audit.m` up to the output paths, one coercion in the export of the kernel generators and the export of the quotient coordinates); this run and `tail_order3_stabilizers.py` with its export (read by `primitive_cubic_test/replay_sage.py`) were not in the evidence list of the record and are added here, so that every replay runs from the repository alone.
* `C2x42`: Several runs export byte-identical files (the genus-5 Hecke data `explicit_hecke_data.txt` of `audit_hecke`, `audit_hecke_v2`, `primitive_cubic_test` and `CM_Y5_retest`, the genus-25 model bases `model_bases.txt` and the cusp audits `cusp_audit.txt` of the `X_1(42)` runs, ...); each is kept under its own name, as the replays read them there (`replay_direct_rr_lattices_sage.py` asserts that the three model bases agree).
* `C2x42`: The direct enumeration `enumerate_direct.m` appended to its export; it now overwrites it (`Overwrite:=true`). It lists its records in an order Magma does not fix, and the later filters use the record indices, so rerun the enumeration only together with the filters, or restore the committed `C2x42/data/M2N42_D9_direct_full*_arithmetic.txt` first.
* `C2x42`: `C2x42/data/tate_cusps.json` is `compute(2,42,5,9)` of `cusp_conditions_relative.py` (noncyclic/cusp_conditions_relative.py of the record), and `cusp_degrees.txt`, read by `preflight.m`, is its list `closed_cusp_degrees`; both were written by inline Python of the Codex session. `preflight.m` was run by an inline launcher with `magma -b` (transcript, 26 October 15:52 UTC; exit 0).
* `C2x42`: The Python launchers `C2x42/run_*.py` and `C2x42/*/run_*.py` are the original supervisors (CPU and memory limits, `magma -b`, exit record); they now call `magma -n -b` from `PATH`, write the logs into `C2x42/logs/`, and no longer register the job with the Codex resource monitor (`register_resource_job.py`, not included); their launch, identity and exit records are not committed. `jobs.txt` runs the same Magma commands directly.
* `C2x42`: The Sage replays read and write their data in `C2x42/data/...` and the plane model of `X_1(42)` in `mdmagma/models_X1_n/FFFc42.txt` (only the path anchors were changed); `CM_cyclic42_A11/replay_finite_norm_sage.py` and `replay_final_norms_sage.py` no longer read the runner exit records (`completion.json`, not kept); they still check the completion lines of the committed logs. `replay_rational_complements_sage.py` runs `genus5_quotient/replay_genus5_sage.py --readonly`, so the genus-5 replay must have been run first (the order of `jobs.txt`).
* `C2x42`: `C2x42/logs/CM_Y5_retest/replay_sage.log` was written by `CM_Y5_retest/replay_sage.py` before an assertion (`b`-stability of the eight CM supports) was added; the current script prints the same text (checked for this repository) and its read-only run is `replay_sage_readonly.log`.
* `C2x42`: `probe_finite.m` writes empty Riemann–Roch identity records `C2x42/logs/genus5_quotient/p5_RR.log`, `p11_RR.log` (no identity is needed); they are not committed.
* `C2x42`: The paused interactive session `setup_cyclic42_session.m` (with `query_cyclic42_*.m`, `hold_native_stdin.py`, `cyclic42_session.log`) and the exploratory folders `cyclic42_scalar/`, `diagonal_genus17/`, `genus5_quotient/{hecke11_pilot,hecke13,prime13}/` of the record feed no evidence and are not included; nothing in this repository was produced by an interactive, paused or killed run.
* `C2x26`: the copied degree-8 run `noncyclic/N26_lower/original_finite_class_data_producer.{m,log}` and its export `F3_class_arithmetic_lower8.txt` of the record are byte copies of `work/degree8/noncyclic/class_2_26_D8.{m,log}` and `N26_D8_class_arithmetic.txt` of the degree-8 record (the same files as `C2x26/class_2_26_D8.m` etc. of octic_torsion); they are kept here under their degree-8 names in `C2x26/degree8/`, `C2x26/logs/degree8/` and `C2x26/data/degree8/`.
* `C2x26`: renamed from the record: `noncyclic/N26_A3/stabilizers.{m,log}` and `stabilizers_arithmetic.txt` are `C2x26/N26_D9_stabilizers.m`, `C2x26/logs/N26_D9_stabilizers.log`, `C2x26/data/N26_D9_stabilizers_arithmetic.txt`; `noncyclic/C226_completion_20261026/class_last2.*` (the last class test of Proposition 11.10) are at the top of `C2x26/`; the Codex packets `C226_occurrence_20261026`, `C226_explicit_20261026` and `C52_completion_20261026` are the sub-folders `occurrence/`, `explicit/` and `C52/`.
* `C2x26`: Magma's `Write` appends; the scripts that export a committed data file (`N26_D9_threequotients.m`, `N26_D9_stabilizers.m`, `N26_D9_last2_Hecke.m`, `class_last2.m`, `degree8/class_2_26_D8.m`, `occurrence/finite_geometric_certificate.m` and `finite_geometric_reproduction.m` for the cuspidal relations, and the three discovery scripts) write with `Overwrite:=true` here. Reruns reproduce every committed data file of `C2x26/data/` byte for byte.
* `C2x26`: `C2x26/N26_D9_stabilizers.m`, `N26_D9_last2_Hecke.m` and `class_last2.m` read `C2x26/data/N26_D9_threequotients_survivors.m`, which `N26_D9_threequotients.m` writes; `discovery/exact_branch_value.m` and `extract_explicit_field.m` read the output of `miller_pencil_function_charzero_driver.m` (and `extract_explicit_field.m` that of `exact_branch_value.m`). The files are identical on a rerun, but with `verify_all.sh -j` these jobs may run at the same time.
* `C2x26`: `occurrence/reproduce.py` and `explicit/reproduce.py` (the fresh reproductions made in the original record) take their output directory as argument; here they are run on `C2x26/logs/occurrence/reproduced_run` and `C2x26/logs/explicit/fresh_reproduction`, they accept an existing directory, and besides `native.log` they write there the wrapper `driver.m`, a `completion.json` and copies of the exported data and of the replay logs, which are not committed (they are byte-identical to the committed ones; `explicit/reproduce.py` checks this for `native_arithmetic.txt`). Their first `Loading` line names the absolute path of the script.
* `C2x26`: `replay_N26_partial_D9.py` (the replay of the Codex N26 packet, which predates the treatment of the last two orbits) ends with `N26_D9_EXCLUSION_PROVED=false`: at that stage orbits 21 and 58 were open; `class_last2.m` and `C52/replay_uniqueness_sage.py` settle them.
* `C2x26`: `discovery/` holds the computation by which `E_0` was found (Codex `C226_completion_20261026`; README: "discovery_note"); its input `data/discovery/weighted_squared_unit_coefficients.txt` was found by exploratory scripts of that folder that are not kept (the Miller function built from it is checked at 3 and 7 to have divisor `A - C`). No proof depends on it.
* `C2x26`: the Python replays were adapted to the layout (data in `C2x26/data/`, the helpers `common/finite_arithmetic_char3.py`, `C2x30/finite_arithmetic.py`, `common/cusp_conditions_relative.py`); `verify_fresh_lower8_prefix.py` takes the log to check as an optional argument (default: the archived prefix).
* `realizations`: `common/data/inputs/LowDegreePlaces` is Mark van Hoeij's data file `LowDegreePlaces` from https://www.math.fsu.edu/~hoeij/files/X1N/ (unchanged copy; its SHA-256 is recorded in `common/data/inputs/vanhoeij_nonic_input.json`); `realizations/extract_nonic_source.py` derives `vanhoeij_nonic_input.json` and `vanhoeij_nonic_input.m` from it, and they are also read by the cases `C33`, `C58_C62` and `C78_C117`.
* `realizations`: `verify_nonic_examples.m` writes `realizations/logs/verify_nonic_examples.log` itself; its standard output, with the `Loading` line, is `verify_nonic_examples_stdout.log` (`verify_nonic_examples.stdout` in the record). Both come from the same run. A second run of a copy of the script in the record (`root_fresh_nonic_examples.*`) gave the same log up to timings and is not included.
* `realizations`: `extract_nonic_source.py` and `prepare_reduction_replay.py` have no log in the record; rerun here, they write `common/data/inputs/vanhoeij_nonic_input.{json,m}` and `realizations/data/selected_reductions.{json,m}` byte for byte. `audit_nonic_realizations.py` was adapted to the layout and no longer writes the Codex packet manifest `realizations_manifest.json` (not included).
* `realizations`: Lemma 2.11 (torsion of CM elliptic curves over nonic fields) is a citation of Clark, Corn, Rice and Stankewicz, Section 4.9, and has no computation; the Codex record for it (`realizations/cm/`: a saved copy of the paper, a text extract of its Section 4.9 and a manifest) is not included.
* `independent`: independent/: the scripts of the independent recomputations were run from their task folders under the review folder; for this repository the load path of `independent/task2/check_entry.m` and the `cd` lines of the three shell drivers were changed so that they run from the repository root (jobs.txt) or from their own folder (run_one.sh, drive.sh). The 344 MB of histograms written by `independent/task4/drive.sh` (folder `independent/task4/hist/`) are not included.

## Hardware

The computations were run on the server Mordell at the University of Zagreb, with an AMD EPYC
9175F CPU (16 cores, 32 threads) and 384 GB of RAM, using Magma V2.30-1. The committed runs of the
original record took about 38 hours of CPU time in total (the CPU times recorded in the
logs and in the runner's exit records, counting interrupted runs up to the interruption), and the
reruns made for this repository about 3.1 hours.
The longest single computations were the export of the Cartier expansions on `X_1(51)` (13183 s;
a computation of the octic record that the proof reuses), the Hecke tests of the noncuspidal
places on `X_1(61)` over `F_2` (6333 s), the single-place types of the octic sieve on `X_1(46)` (6044 s; the
whole degree-9 sieve, rerun for this repository, took 2166 s) and the octic sieve on `X_1(44)`
(5838 s), the decomposition of the j-fibres on the genus-45 quotient of `X_1(67)` (5400 s until it
was killed in an optional final step; the rerun without that step took 2171 s), the model and
formal-immersion test on the quotient of `X_1(133)` (4737 s; the clean rerun 2902 s), the Cartier
export of the mixed case of `Z/49` (4676 s) and the maps to `X_1(40)` for `Z/2 × Z/40` (4419 s).
Runs were given memory limits of 2–64 GB, and one of 128 GB (an octic computation of `Z/48`). The
independent recomputations of the review (`independent/`) took about 0.2 hours of CPU
time in Magma, apart from the PARI/GP enumeration of task 4.

The code written for these computations is released under the MIT license (see `LICENSE`), with
the exceptions below. The submodules carry their own licenses.

The following files are not (only) our own code; their licences take precedence over the MIT
license, and the author has to check that they may be distributed here:

* `prime_bound/DKSS_original.magma` is a verbatim copy of the Magma code of Derickx, Kamienny,
  Stein and Stoll that accompanies *Torsion points on elliptic curves over number fields of small
  degree*, distributed under the GNU GPL version 3 (`prime_bound/DKSS_LICENSE`). It is not run; it
  is the source of the functions `kamiennyH`/`genH` that are copied, with changes, into
  `prime_bound/prime_FI_certificate.m`, `P37/allbad_37_export.m`, `P41_P47_P59/allbad_41_47_export.m`,
  `P41_P47_P59/allbad_59_export.m`, `P43_P53_P61/allbad_43_export.m`,
  `P43_P53_P61/p53_allbad_FI_export.m`, `common/allbad_61_67_71_export.m`, `P73/allbad_73_export.m` and
  `common/allbad_79_83_89_export.m`. These scripts are therefore derived works of GPL code and are
  distributed under the GNU GPL version 3 (a copy of the licence sits in each of these folders).
* `prime_bound/strange_primes_original.magma` is a verbatim copy of the Magma code accompanying
  Derickx and Stoll, *Prime order torsion on elliptic curves over number fields, Part I:
  Asymptotics* (the determination of the strange primes); `prime_bound/verify_prime_bound.py` checks
  the table of that paper against it. Its licence is not stated in the file.
* `common/dt_expansions_local.m` is derived verbatim from the CurveArith package of Derickx and Terao
  (commit `0263b343`, `src/gonality.m`) and is distributed under the GNU GPL version 3, whose text is
  `common/GPL3_LICENSE`.
* `common/class_group_unflattened.m` and `common/native_group_generators.m` contain functions copied
  verbatim from Magma's package file `Ring/FldFun/classgroup.m` (V2.30-1; `native_group_generators.m`
  is `EvalDataGenerators`, lines 699–740, without a profiling call); the MIT license does not apply
  to them.
* Data: `common/data/inputs/LowDegreePlaces` and `common/data/C35/gonality` are Mark van Hoeij's
  (<https://www.math.fsu.edu/~hoeij/files/X1N/>), `common/data/lmfdb_newforms_cache.json` comes from
  the LMFDB, `common/data/dcm_list_all_min_orders_X1_10k.m` from the least-cm-degree repository of
  Clark, Genao, Pollack and Saia, and `prime_bound/data/derickx_stoll_table2.txt` is Table 2 of
  Derickx–Stoll, extracted from the arXiv version of the paper.
